This shit is fire b1Start with HOOK at 0:00 (no pre-roll). Hook must be byte-identical each repeat. Hook design: call-and-response, stacked chants, minimal melisma; phrasing fits 8-bar loop cleanly. Lead tone: upfront, breathy grit → confident belt on hook; de-esser gentle; consonants articulate. Arrangement: HOOK → Verse 1 → Pre → HOOK → Verse 2 (rap speed-switch) → Break → Final HOOK (extended). Timbre: drums punch; sub clean; synths restrained; horns only in hook; crowd up in hook tails. FX & Edits: barline-aligned mutes; tape stop ONLY at bar ends; slam-backs post-line, never mid-phrase. Mix: radio width without smearing center; +1 dB vocal vs. beat; sidechain pads under vocal lines. Constraints: no “poem” line wrapping; bars read like lead sheet; keep adlibs off stressed syllables.

Confident rapper delivers auto-tuned, multi-layered vocals, building to an anthemic, hard-hitting chorus with a dark, moody bounce.

Jack RightousThis gangsta ass zydeco-trap infused with country-mumble rap storytelling. we making our own genre Suno! Opening with syncopated accordion riffs and rhythmic washboard over a grooving bass, the verse sets a rootsy tone, Country trap beats and 808s join, blending banjo licks and acoustic guitar, The female lead’s vocals glide atop, with call-and-response harmonies and a danceable, driving groove. Jack Rightous (Remix) is a commercial-ready track created by MoMoney Studios in collaboration with Suno. What began as a straightforward “how-to” AI tutorial has been transformed into a sermon-like anthem — gospel cadences over trap grit with DJ-cut swagger. It’s a nod to the source, but also a declaration: Suno enables, MoMoney Studios elevates. Together, they turn raw words into viral records.

Happy (Remix)The track launches with widened pads and stacked harmonies panned ±16° for Chorus 1, while a prominent 808—processed through a low-sub bus, subtly sidechained to the kick and blended with upright bass—adds punch. An ethereal arpeggiated synth, treated with tape chorus and drenched in reverb mid-chorus, floats above. In Verse 2, airy pads evolve, thin harmonic textures emerge, and vocals thicken with a tight double and precise stutter FX on “deep.” Chorus 2 intensifies: anthem-like, drums and 808 are parallel-compressed, low-mids fill out, harmonies soar. For the Bridge, 808 drops out; vocals, sparse guitar, and ambient pads dominate, heightening vulnerability before a gradual build with pulsating bass. The outro is stripped and warm: lead repeats the title, 808 sustains, and layers decay through tape-stop and vinyl-static FX while the spatial field gently closes.

Happy (Remix)During the Bridge, drop the 808 entirely for the first half to expose lyrical vulnerability. Strip back to vocals, minimal guitar, and pad; then reintroduce the deep pulse under the second half for an emotional lift. For the Outro, let the lead repeat the title phrase with progressively less intensity while the 808 sustains. Automate an analog tape-stop effect in the final two bars and sprinkle controlled vinyl-static artifacts on the tail to leave the listener in a searching, unresolved space. Throughout, favor analog saturation, controlled dynamics, and intentional negative space—exclude bright EDM arpeggios, glossy auto-tune, and wide chorus effects that would blur the acoustic textures.

Happy (Remix)For Chorus 1, widen the pad and pan two stacked vocal harmonies ± 16°. Bring the 808 forward on its own low-sub bus, sidechained gently to the kick so it coexists with the upright bass. Introduce an ethereal synth arpeggio in the upper register, processed with tape chorus and a long reverb tail only on the second half of each bar. In Verse 2, let the pads evolve by adding a thin harmonic shimmer and a second vocal double 10–20 ms behind the lead for analog thickness. Vary the “deep” stutter rhythmically so it becomes part of the melodic hook. Chorus 2 should feel anthem-like: push parallel compression harder on the drum/808 bus, boost the low-mid for warmth, and raise the harmonies slightly without obscuring the lead.

Happy (Remix)The track opens with a focused, tape-saturated DJ Drop vocal stem, hotly tracked with lows cut at 120Hz, loop-chopped and dressed in 1/8-note gated reverb for sampled club punch. A narrow analog pad enters, filtered down from 300Hz to 80Hz as an 808 pulse booms in on bar 4, with tape wow/flutter for vintage vibe. Verse 1 strips to close-mic’d layered acoustic guitars (one fingerpicked, one palm-muted), brush kit via ribbon mics, upright bass DI plus room, and a behind-the-beat syncopated 1/16th hi-hat. Lead vocal is dry, tape-warmed, short plate; pockets stay intimate and organic.

Diamond are 4ever (client) (Remix)This chopped and screwed crunk remix kicks off with thunderous 808 bass, relentless hi-hat trills, and heavy, swirling synth layers. Pitched-down, fragmented vocals feature double stutters, expert scratching, sudden breaks, and dynamic x2/x4 loops. Houston FX and reversed female hooks drive a hypnotic, sub-heavy groove.

Diamond are 4ever (baseline)This neo-"mothership connection" P-Funk remix layer serves an evolutionary layer of the original track. Think live DJ jam kicks off with thick slap bass, layered clavinet, and cosmic synth stabs. Horns punch syncopated riffs while wah-wah guitars trade lines. Percussion is tight yet wild, driving deep grooves as vocal chants and group call-and-response pump the energy.

Diamond are 4ever (#MoKnowsRemix)This neo-"mothership connection" P-Funk jam kicks off with thick slap bass, layered clavinet, and cosmic synth stabs. Horns punch syncopated riffs while wah-wah guitars trade lines. Percussion is tight yet wild, driving deep grooves as vocal chants and group call-and-response pump the energy.

Vanity's Seed (BlackOnBLonde Remix)A neo-Soul/Southern Soul FUSED with strip club-gospel-country-trap Southern Gothic soundscape opens with Hammond organ drones, muted upright piano, syncopated stomp loops, and vinyl crackle. Melismatic all-female vocals blend whispers and chest-depth gospel shouts, surging to 164 BPM double-time with handclaps, sub-bass, syncopated percussion, and wide stereo call-and-response gospel choir. Preacher echoes and sus9/add11 gospel chords heighten tension. Verses are lo-fi mono, bridges fracture from whispers to shouts over organ pads and tape hiss with choir bleed. Choruses bloom stereo-wide with pedal tones, trailing lush reverb and grief into silence-fade. Vocals feature 5-octave range, deep microtonal melisma, syrupy chest/head transitions, and vocal fry.

Accident (Clapback) (Remix)Thick, syrupy Houston trill production anchors the track with rolling 808s and wide, rubbery sub-bass designed to flex windshields. Beat rides a chopped-and-screwed groove, crisp hi-hats, and trunk-rattling kicks. Swirling synth layers, moody bells, and wah guitars add swagger. Hooks drop with unfiltered, super-slowed vocal stabs. Club-ready, head-nodding, and built to shake the block.

How Great Thou Art (#MoKnowsGod)A country-trap Southern Gothic soundscape unfolds with Hammond drones, muted upright piano, syncopated stomp loops, and vinyl crackle, setting a foggy hush. Club Anthem-TikTok ViRAL featuring only female vocals blend melisma and whispers, expanding to gospel shouts. Double-time surges to 164 BPM with rapid Southern flow, handclaps, percussion, and call-and-response gospel choir in wide stereo, layered with preacher echoes and sub-bass. Gospel sus9/add11 chords linger for tension, bridges fracture with whisper-to-shout dynamics over organ pads, tape hiss mixing with choir bleed. Verses are lo-fi mono, choruses swell wide with pedal tones and reverb trailing grief into a silence-fade.

ai freestyle (suno and mo/$) (Remix)This remix layer serves as an evolution of the existing arrangement: a Houston chopped and screwed process slowing tempo to 60–70 bpm, a percentage of original speed that bends pitch naturally. Vocals are shifted down an extra -4 to -8 semitones based on range, from soprano to deep baritone. Chops are created by slicing phrases and layering duplicates with slight delay, simulating dual turntables. Added drag is evoked by screwing down the arm with a penny, mimicking original DIY methods. The result is syrup-slow groove with pitched-down resonance, staggered repeats, vinyl crackle, spinbacks, stutters, echo-drenched ad-libs, and a hypnotic, intoxicated atmosphere.

INERTIA (#MoKnowsRemix)Imagine neo-"mothership connection" type pfunk jam session. with me telling the staff i just want the track to get funked up cause theres a whole lot of rythm going round,

inertiaA chopped-and-screwed cipher-soul session in D minor at 92 BPM slowed to 46 BPM syrup feel. Bunny SNO, a 22-year-old cipher spitter with a two-octave range, flips double-speed triplet flows into halved, dragged phrases. Verses extend from 8 to 16 bars using chopped repeats on bars 4, 8, 12, and 16, doubling phrases without breaking the beat matrix. Hook layers are stretched with quad chops and drag-slurs, creating hypnotic echoes of her own words. DJ MoMoney introduces with an 8-bar drop and shoutouts resurface mid-verse. Timbre blends contralto belts, chopped cipher vocals, slowed 808s, vinyl hiss, Hammond pads, and gospel echoes. Performance pivots between rapid spit, syrup-drag chops, and soulful lifts, maintaining grid alignment while evoking Houston cassette culture. The mood is slowed, defiant, and haunting — a testimony of cipher grit pulled into the Southern screwed dimension.

Amazing Grace (#MoKnowsBass) v1This remix layer serves as an evolution of the sound of Amazing Grace. Slow everything to a syrupy groove in classic Chop & Screw tradition—channel Houston’s legacy by pitching vocals and 808s down, stretching tempo to half-speed at key gospel hooks, Use AAVN pronunciation throughout, extend all melismatic runs, and layer soulful preacher-style mumble rap with drawn-out gospel choir responses, Employ LoopSlice on every “Come on in the room” chorus, ScrubStutter on high-energy phrases, and Scratch vinyl FX during transitions, Organ swells and hi-hats should drag with lush reverb tails, Powerhouse altos and soaring sopranos should deliver overlapping, echoed adlibs, building hypnotic layers of gospel intensity, Create a dreamy, church-at-midnight atmosphere with call-and-response, chopped vocal stacks, and extreme time-stretch on emotional peaks, For the final outro, let congregation shouts, preacher adlibs, and slow-motion handclaps swirl in a deep, reverent, reverb-drenched fade

Grit over gloss. Southern gothic intensity.”

Amazing Grace (Layer 3: Pops)This is the next to last of our remix layers. Onced again we are simply enhancing the depth and width of our songs musicality via personas that we treat like "plug-ins" in a DAW. A Trap Soul instrumental in E Aeolian (with Dorian color), 4/4 swung at 76 BPM, opens with felt upright piano weaving a conscience motif. Detuned Rhodes and airy pads create a neon-church haze, layered with tape hiss. Alto sax sings the main theme, answered by tenor and baritone sax for rich harmonic stacks. Percussion blends trap kit grooves with textural fills, while valve tenor trombone and silver Bach bass add cathartic stabs and harmonic tension. The bridge slows to 72 BPM, teasing major lifts before a soulful drop, building to emotional release in the outro as ensemble trade motifs, evoking a gospel-meets-desert bar aura.

Bridge surges via pedal steel/harmonica duet, breaks into Memphis horn stabs, then a sax-trombone duel. Finale fuses both voices in rich gospel textures.

Getaway v4.5+ (Remix)This Country-Trap Gospel Noir session rides a swung halftime bounce at 74 BPM, blending tape-recorded acoustic grit—banjo, lap-steel, upright bass—with Southern revival and swampy gospel. Gravel baritone preacher leads, barbed by falsetto and punctuated preacher shouts, with a male quartet and gospel choir answering in complex, layered call-and-response. Hammond organ with Leslie and tribal percussion build dramatic lift, while 808 and stomp-claps lock deep groove. Lo-fi intro with heartbeat and vinyl crackle yields to confessional, dry verses; plate-reverb swells elevate choir-laden hooks. Textures shift: reverse-verb, chopped-and-screwed echoes, and rap cameo over a syncopated Texas bounce. Bridge leaps up +2 semitones for peak energy, rushing into an explosive choir-driven hook. The outro drags the chopped elements into a slow, haunted fade; mono sub and crispy, hard-clipped drums anchor the analog-warmth and stereo choir pads.

INERTIA (MoMoney Studios Remix)The song opens with lo-fi Rhodes, tape hiss, and ambient gospel pads under a crackling vinyl bed. Dusty 808s and vinyl-warped snares set a swung groove; mono sub bass hugs below, widening above 180 Hz. Contralto vocals, haunted then coldly triumphant, ride gospel stacks and whisper overlays. Slide guitar sighs mingle with banjo echoes and shamisen plucks, punctuated by ad-lib overlap, consonant snaps, and blooming vowels. FX—wind hums, distant thunder, and rain—rise through harmonic stacks, deepening the emotional tension and release, guiding from neon-lit church to smoky desert bar.

cant - magSouthern Soul/Dancehall Trap structure driven by tape-recorded acoustic vibes. Raw floor toms, handclaps, and heavy bass underpin a deep contralto bursting with AAVN, microtonality, vocal fry, and smokey tones. Sparse, distorted slide guitar counterpoints verses; chains and metal hits add grit. Choruses ignite with powerful shouts and blasting guitar, while the climax grows dense—stacked percussion, saturated instruments, and vocals unleashed in a cathartic, chaotic release.

cant magThis Southern Soul/Bluesy R&B track unfolds as a tape-recorded acoustic session. A deep contralto female vocalist commands attention, weaving AAVN, microtonal inflections, vocal fry, and smokey, almost-tenor emotive runs. Her delivery is sassy, confident, and smooth, remaining front & center, locked in the pocket. The journey begins with an intimate, whispered intro over ambient pads, sub-bass, and sparse, cracking trap hi-hats. Testimony I introduces a rhythmic flow with tight 808 kick/snare, banjo loop, and subtle synth chords, maintaining a luxury bounce. Testimony II builds power with heavy 808s and lush gospel pads, featuring a pronounced southern vocal drawl. The Ancestral Bridge swells with a falsetto lead, rich gospel harmonies, pedal steel, and church organ. A raw, vulnerable spoken-word confessional unfolds over a lo-fi banjo loop. The release section explodes with dual female harmonies, full power vocals, chopped gospel samples, and high-energy trap fusion. The outro fades

How Deep... (RemixSuno Flex)This chopped and screwed crunk remix launches with deep 808s and rapid hi-hat trills layered under swirling, thick synths. Vocals are pitched down, slow, and diced with double stutters, expert scratching, abrupt stops, and x2/x4 looping. Houston FX and reversed female hooks add hypnotic edge.

I don’t chase, I move in light v0aG.L.O.W. v0.1 (Remix) (Remastered) (Remix)This Southern Soul/Bluesy R&B track unfolds as a tape-recorded acoustic session. A deep contralto female vocalist commands attention, weaving AAVN, microtonal inflections, vocal fry, and smokey, almost-tenor emotive runs. Her delivery is sassy, confident, and smooth, remaining front & center, locked in the pocket. The journey begins with an intimate, whispered intro over ambient pads, sub-bass, and sparse, cracking trap hi-hats. Testimony I introduces a rhythmic flow with tight 808 kick/snare, banjo loop, and subtle synth chords, maintaining a luxury bounce. Testimony II builds power with heavy 808s and lush gospel pads, featuring a pronounced southern vocal drawl. The Ancestral Bridge swells with a falsetto lead, rich gospel harmonies, pedal steel, and church organ. A raw, vulnerable spoken-word confessional unfolds over a lo-fi banjo loop. The release section explodes with dual female harmonies, full power vocals, chopped gospel samples, and high-energy trap fusion. The outro fades

Glow Don’t Run (Trailer Park Tabernacle Mix)This track fuses Southern Americana, Gospel Roots, Acoustic Blues, and Americana grit over a tight, syncopated pocket with tribal-infused acoustic percussion. Warm upright bass, layered acoustic guitars, muted slide, keys/pads, and brush kit build a wide, clean stereo field. The mix balances raw, emotive low tenor vocals—centered and front—with professional analog detail and live session energy, preserving organic interplay and authentic grit throughout.

Banjo and Hammond/Leslie trade phrases; muted slide provides “switchblade” scrapes. Sections and voices declared in [Voice] with crew ad-libs; road-map embedded as section_names. Verses stay dry and close; hooks widen via parallel K/S and 1.2 s plate with tenor stack. Texas-bounce Rap Break uses chopped vinyl ticks; Pre-Chorus stacks quartet harmonies; Bridge lifts key +2 with cinematic sub-drop and full choir wave. Final Chorus belts, then executes half-speed chopped repeat into hard tape-stop; Outro leaves porch-room ring. Master to −12 LUFS; tape emu; no glossy chorus or EDM arps.

Intro sets porch-revival mood: crowd hush, stomp-clap, spoons. Verse 1 keeps vocals dry and intimate; organ side-chains to kick. Chorus widens with plate 1.2 s and brass stabs; tenor stack lifts hooks. Verse 2 answers banjo with organ swells. Rap Break shifts to double-time swagger, chopped vinyl accents. Pre-Chorus brings quartet build. Bridge modulates +2 semitones with cinematic sub-drop and full choir wave. Final Chorus belts, then half-speed chopped repeat into tape-stop. Outro returns to banjo and whispered amen. Analog saturation and tape emu throughout; no glossy chorus.

Day 3+10 - Oh No Its Suno and Mo (Can You Stand The Rain) (Remix)This remix opens with a thunderous bass drop and crisp 808 kicks. The rhythm section piles layered hi-hats and claps for an infectious strip club bounce. Heavy synth stabs and a warbling sub bass anchor the groove. For 16 bars, advanced DJ turntablism explodes—scratches, crab walks, and wild chop and screw FX warp vocals and samples, punctuated by stuttered stops and dramatic tempo/pitch bends before dropping back into the main booty-shaking groove.

Poster 2 — 151bpm (Remix)A country-trap Southern Gothic soundscape unfolds with Hammond drones, muted upright piano, syncopated stomp loops, and vinyl crackle, setting a foggy hush. Club Anthem-TikTok ViRAL featuring only female vocals blend melisma and whispers, expanding to gospel shouts. Double-time surges to 164 BPM with rapid Southern flow, handclaps, percussion, and call-and-response gospel choir in wide stereo, layered with preacher echoes and sub-bass. Gospel sus9/add11 chords linger for tension, bridges fracture with whisper-to-shout dynamics over organ pads, tape hiss mixing with choir bleed. Verses are lo-fi mono, choruses swell wide with pedal tones and reverb trailing grief into a silence-fade.

Poster 2 — 151bpm (MoMoney Remix)A rebellious collaborator take on Buddah Bless — “Poster 2 — 151bpm.” Keep the original bell‑lead ostinato bright and upfront, with punchy kick, sharp snare, and the simple, repetitive sub foundation breathing under a clean vocal pocket. Verses sit on‑grid with precise consonants; Verse 2 delivers a controlled double‑time display for contrast, then flips to a brief half‑time screw feel with chopped, looped chant phrases. The bridge gives a tiny screw nod (two bars) before snapping back to the grid. Energy arc: manifesto intro → 16‑bar Verse 1 (origin lens) → chant hook → 16‑bar Verse 2 (double‑time → half‑time with loops) → micro screw bridge → 16‑bar Verse 3 (meta lens) → final chant hook with call‑and‑response twist (repeat once) → dry tag. Texture stays uncluttered; any ear‑candy is minimal (tiny transition glitches, slight plate on hook). Tone: defiant, inclusive, polished. Vocals forward; bell bright; drums tight.

Poster 2 — 151bpm (Remix)Opening with a polyrhythmic southern trap beat, chopped Latin percussion, deep sub-bass, and razor-sharp inverted koto stabs as a countermelody syncopated and slighly behind the vocal pocket, each verse showcases region-specific sounds: Texas accordion licks, Gullah guitar, Japanese tonalities, Afrobeat interplay, and Aussie pop harmonies, Distinctive vocal deliveries cut through shifting grooves, culminating in a striking a cappella breakdown before roaring back into the power-packed beat for a united, energetic finale.

The track unfolds into a layered gospel chorus with call-and-response harmonies, supported by brushed drums, gospel organ, and reverb-heavy pads. Vinyl crackle and subtle tape wobble add a vintage intimacy. Feels like a Sunday morning memory wrapped in analog warmth, filled with soul, struggle, and redemption. *** Give me Memphis Horn Session and a Saxophone/trombone duel in the breakdown....

Whiskey Shadows and Wounds i2A Southern Gothic country-trap ballad unfolds in a fog of vinyl crackle and pedal steel swells, muted banjo plucks tracing the edges of a whispered female confession. At 82 BPM, the groove is anchored by 808 thump and a tight, inverted percussion pocket, micro-delayed to push the beat forward while pedal steel, banjo, and Hammond pads weave a counter-melody. The male verses arrive in melodic Southern AAVN cadence—half rap, half blues lament—while the female choruses bloom into wide stereo with gospel melisma and pedal-tone harmonies. Harmonica accents ghost between phrases, and gospel sus9/add11 pivots hang like unfinished prayers. Verses are lo-fi mono compressed for intimacy, with tape hiss hugging the words; choruses widen into plate reverb swells and deep subbass weight. The bridge rises on harmonica-pedal steel duet under a soaring female belt, pulling into a final chorus where both voices lock in dual harmony, the choir swelling in the distance. It ends on a harmonica sustain.

Can YOu Stand The Rain (Suno and Mo Remix) (Instrumental) (Remix)The track opens in A minor, 82 BPM, riding a 4/4 pocket with soft swing. Chord changes pulse every two bars, shifting from Am7 into a soulful Dm9, before resolving into G13 extensions that create both tension and uplift. The intro floats with suspended chords, anchoring nothing — setting the emotional unease that follows. A contralto vocalist enters slow, smoky — voice laid-back but cutting, each line pulsing with diaphragm tension and breathy vibrato. She's grounded in Southern gospel but flows like R&B. The mic catches every grain. Adlibs don't flare — they hover, ghosting the lead like memory. Then the rapper jumps in, all bounce and blade. Her flow punches — double-time to half-time flips mid-bar, ricocheting off the trap snare. R&B progression and sub-bass cradle her voice like memory. She sings from stillness, not sadness — this is storm-watching soul. Chordal swells rise behind percussive handclaps. Her rhythm gets syncopated, her voice starts asking questions.

Hank F&F Test in Cant Lose (Remix)charleston south carolina born, spent childhood and school years in five-points area of columbia on weekends soaking up bands. went to university of georgia and spent weekends in athens, ga and pigeon forge, tn juke joints and bars. moved to austin, tx for two years working 5th street bars during the week (except wednesday nights he went to houston blues society jam sessions hosted at the big easy social club learning from thomas) and driving up to D/FW on weekends. influences: lynard skynard, hank jr, houston trill (loves dj screw and danjah ra from mddlfngz), soul train on saturday mornings and hee haw on saturday nights, top 40 radio show on sundays after church, and lawerence welk on sunday nights at his grandparents. Musicians He's Studied: Mentor: Luther and The Healers (Houston, TX) Guitar: Robert Johnson, Woodwinds: Hans and Candy Dulfer

i can't lose (we gone global!) (Remix)Multi-deck offset looping – Load the same track on two (or more) turntables with a one-beat offset, then slam the crossfader side-to-side to repeat phrases without interrupting the tempo. – Pull records back manually for unpredictable chops, thinking in “slow-motion” to land precise cuts and stutters4. Dynamic pitch-drift mapping – Automate gradual pitch-down of –4 semitones over 15–20% of the track to accentuate the “screwed” feel. – Map slow-attack curves on your 808s so sub-bass remains weighty even when sliced and stuttered. Reverse-texture breakdowns – Apply phase inversion and reverse effects to vocal or synth stems during drops for disorienting build-ups. – Use long-tail reverb on chopped vocal leads to glue reversed textures into the mix. Advanced scratch-chop hybrids – Combine turntable scratching techniques (flare, crab, orbit) with chopped loops: trigger stuttered phrases then record them into your DAW for further manipulation. – Introduce stop-time tactics by automating

how deep 713-420-2234 cover by harry(Remix) (Remix)Syrupy chopped-and-screwed hip hop underpins with thick, massively slowed 808s and scattershot hi-hat trills. Vocals are deeply pitched: baritone male and dark contralto female, both affected by tempo/pitch shifts. Verses float over pulsing sub-bass; the hook fractures into fourfold vocal stutters, hit by slow-attack sub drops and reversed textures. Scratch fills punctuate throughout, with pitch-drifted loops emphasizing the ‘how’ motif. FX splash with flare cuts and cavernous delays, keeping the vibe dense, raw, and nocturnal. Imagine cruising the neon-lit freeway at 2 AM: syrup-thick 808s pulse, hi-hat trills swirl, and pitched-down vocals melt into reversed breakdowns. - Chop the hook into 4× stutters, apply a slow-attack sub curve, then cue flare scratches on the freestyle phrase. - Loop the phrase ‘how… how… how deep is your love’ 4× with 2 s gaps, then loop the word ‘how’ 2× at 1 s intervals. - Encourage raw energy: ‘You can freestyle—own each bar!’

how deep is your loveImagine if the royal purple-wearing legend from Minneapolis were still alive, reinventing his life to match today's standards, blending the essence of Rag and Bone Man meets the Dulfers in a dark houston blues bar.

Granny's Porch Jam v2A radio-ready Americana fusion, this track spotlights raw, low tenor vocals front and center, layered atop upright bass, syncopated acoustic drums with brushes, and driving tribal percussion. Acoustic guitars (layered and muted slide) meld with subtle keys/pads, delivering tight, emotional grooves in a wide, analog stereo mix. The arrangement flows with dynamics—each section building and dropping with crafted intensity—preserving a live, intimate, and authentically gritty acoustic sound.

Skeet 3 i1A neo-trap battle starts with chopped southern acoustic drums—sticks, cajón, handclaps—on moody acoustic guitar and pads, led by deep 808 bass and punchy kicks. Rapper 1 rides crisp wood snares, kalimba synths, vinyl spin FX, vocal stabs, DJ adlibs, crowd call-response. Rapper 2 brings bouncy snares, reverbed hats, layered adlibs. The final chorus bursts with massed vocal samples, wild crowd chants, and aggressive live scratches using found objects on vinyl, capturing unplugged Southern-Carolina swagger.

Skeet 1.3 5050``` [Instrumental] Acoustic Houston freestyle battle beat: country-trap, Southern Rock, Gospel, AAVN Funk, Delta Blues. Confident, paranoid vibe. Acoustic guitars, soulful acoustic piano, and raw, percussive tribal drums. The track is built for a rapper's flow, establishing the beat count and a head-nodding groove by the second bar. Tribal drums and percussion (hats, snare) emphasize the upbeat, falling slightly behind the beat by approximately 1/16th. [Intro] (4 bars) A clear, immediate rhythmic pattern from tribal drums establishes the count. Ethereal acoustic guitar pads and a twangy, acoustic guitar lick hint at the mood. A deep, resonant acoustic bassline or low-end percussion rumble kicks in crisply. [Verse] (16 bars) Dynamic, acoustic tribal beat. Incorporate strategic breaks, short cutouts, or moments of silence (e.g., for 1-1.5 bars) to create tension and release. Skeet 1.2[Instrumental] Houston freestyle battle beat: country-trap, Southern Rock, Gospel, AAVN Funk, Delta Blues. Confident, paranoid vibe. Deep 808s, gritty guitar, soulful organ, blues harmonica. [Intro] (4 bars) Eerie pads, twangy guitar, deep 808 rumble. [Verse] (16 bars) Heavy trap beat, southern rock guitar, bluesy harmonica. [Pre-Chorus] (4 bars) Tension build, gospel piano, snare roll. [Hook] (8 bars) AAVN funk groove, prominent bass, crisp percussion, melodic synth, delta blues guitar. Anthemic. [Verse] (16 bars) Core beat, varied guitar, gospel organ. [Pre-Chorus] (4 bars) Tension build. [Hook] (8 bars) Powerful statement. [Breakdown] (4 bars) Sparse 808s, haunting pad, wailing blues guitar. [Verse] (16 bars) Renewed energy, aggressive hi-hat. [Hook] (8 bars) Max impact. [Outro] (4 bars) Fading 808s, lingering distorted guitar.

Amazing Grace (radio)This track opens with slide guitar and hiss the rhythm pocketed with stomp-clap energy and a slow Southern swing The lead voice enters dry and reverent a low tenor shaped by time trauma and grace backed by Hammond organ and upright bass The verses remain fully intact delivered bar for bar with gospel phrasing Each section builds slowly from solo to layered harmony keeping the sacred fire alive without gloss The final verse closes with tape hiss and a preacher echo reminding us grace is still strong in the ones who barely made it

Still Here (Remix)Rooted in Southern rock, blues, and hip hop, start with a crunchy slide guitar riff, organ swells, and fat bass. Add subtle chopped-and-screwed trap hats. Verses layer melodic saxophone, blues guitar licks, and vocal stacks. Choruses expand with gospel BGVs and brass. Bridge drops into a syrupy triplet groove, fusing classic Southern flare with modern Trill texture.

bluefunk live v4Opening with a tight, syncopated funk groove—electric bass locked with crisp modern drum programming—layered atop neon-tinged synths and sharp guitar stabs. Verses alternate between falsetto croon and rhythmic spoken delivery, building into a hook laced with lush harmonies. Bridge breaks down into sparse chords and trap hi-hats before exploding back into the infectious, dance-driven groove for the final chorus.

thats my fam (Remix)Begin with an ambient Houston club feel, purple-hued synth pads, and a DJ live-mixing two decks—Deck B cued ahead. Deck A drops vocals, then hard-cut to Deck B, looped and accented with rapid “chirp” scratches. Downbeat-triggered fog enhances the head-nodding, syrupy chopped & screwed beat. Over eight bars, tempo slows 20% and vocals drop 10 semitones; swirling low-pass “syrup drip” FX bind transitions. Midtrack, hit a tape-stop bridge, then backspin to launch a doubled female ad-lib loop. Juggle 1/16th beat kick/snare fills and weave call-and-response between bass-heavy hooks and chopped male samples. Every 16 bars, end with a dramatic turntable backspin, beat drops and melts under a fading spotlight.

Two Truths (Remix) mov1A festival-ready extended "booty shaker" krunk remix fuses deep chopped & screwed FX with syrupy, seductive contralto (almost tenor) deep female vocals (filled with grit from 29 years if smoke and whiskey) channeling southern rock power ballad flair. Throbbing 808s, crisp hi-hats, twangy outlaw country guitar riffs, and pulsing bass lines intertwine under hazy synths, anchoring sensual vocal runs atop slow, pitched-down grooves, plenty of call-and-response crowd moments, layered breakdowns, and bold drops for wild festival energy.

A Change Gonna Come (Cream On My Coffee Remix) (Remastered) (Remix)An extended "booty shaker" krunk remix kicks off with pounding 808s, rattling hi-hats, and thick, swung Houston drip bass. Chopped & screwed effects swirl through the mix: pitched-down ad libs, syrup-slow DJ breaks, and reversed snares. Sultry, syrupy female vocals slide seductively over the groove, punctuated by playful chops and drawn-out echoes. Synth stabs join in on the hook, while breakdowns highlight hypnotic vocal loops, creating a steamy, club-ready atmosphere.

Come On In The Room - CSv0.i3.2 (Remix)Slow everything to a syrupy groove in classic Chop & Screw tradition—channel Houston’s legacy by pitching vocals and 808s down, stretching tempo to half-speed at key gospel hooks. Use AAVN pronunciation throughout, extend all melismatic runs, and layer soulful preacher-style mumble rap with drawn-out gospel choir responses. Employ LoopSlice on every “Come on in the room” chorus, ScrubStutter on high-energy phrases, and Scratch vinyl FX during transitions. Organ swells and hi-hats should drag with lush reverb tails. Powerhouse altos and soaring sopranos should deliver overlapping, echoed adlibs, building hypnotic layers of gospel intensity. Create a dreamy, church-at-midnight atmosphere with call-and-response, chopped vocal stacks, and extreme time-stretch on emotional peaks. For the final outro, let congregation shouts, preacher adlibs, and slow-motion handclaps swirl in a deep, reverent, reverb-drenched fade.

Night Dont End (whiskey shadows and wounds pt. 2)- Syncopated afrobeat grooves blend seamlessly with trap beats and hood rap energy, creating a dynamic fusion, Layered handclaps, finger snaps, and scratch flares interplay with distorted kick drums and glitchy hi-hats, - Deep, sultry female vocals deliver charismatic bars, contrasting with octave-split vocal doubling, rhythmic exhales, and bold adlibs, Raw call-and-response moments merge with whisper throws, adding emotional depth, - Experimental electronic textures and dissonant synth harmonies enhance the atmosphere, while polished EQ, crisp compression, and inventive FX build layers of dimension, - Side-chained kicks and warped bass lines drive the rhythm, locking together in an epic, genre-blurring soundscape, Punchy modern mastering ensures clarity and impact throughout

Ain't No Accident (Clapback chopped) v4.5.0.a (Remix)This crunk Chop and Screwed remix opens with heavy 808s and rolling hi-hat trills, immediately slowing the vibe by 15-20%. Layers of phased synths with thick Houston effects swirl throughout. Vocals are pitched down -4, chopped, looped x2/x4, stuttered, backmasked, laced with advanced scratches and abrupt stops. Syrupy female leads ride the breakdowns as everything drips with signature "chopped not slopped" textures.

Strippin’ & Sippin’: Voice in the Dark (Extended Alchemist Cut) (Remastered) (Remix)A moody downtempo electronic soul track, opening with warm analog synth pads and syncopated trap drums. Soulful reverb-soaked vocals ride over saturated bass, sidechained rhodes, crisp hi-hats, and handclaps. Swelling synths and filtered FX lead to lush keys, live drums, and gliding auto-tuned R&B harmonies in the chorus. The bridge pares down to bass and airy textures before a final dynamic chorus, ending with a gentle wash of keys and echoed vocals.

Just You and Me v2 80-75-50A Houston Trill-influenced gospel track unfolds with a drastically slowed tempo, heavy pitched-down vocals, and dense gospel harmonies forming lush atmospheric pads. The bass and organ are amplified, anchoring the groove, while chopped beats, vinyl scratches, and phased/flanged effects swirl through each section. A raw, deep female vocal leads, accompanied by spontaneous crowd singing and an unpolished texture. Midway, an inventive beat switch shifts the rhythmic direction, deepening the rebellious, meditative mood.

Just You and Me v2.0.a.r3Country Trap meets O.G. Boom Bap in Aeolian mode (A minor), 4/4, Vinyl crackle, chatter, and sparse slide-guitar moans set the scene. Verses blend heavy 808s, sub-bass stutters, UK-drill hats, nonlinear trap grooves; honky-tonk piano drives minor iv–VI, smoky organ swells beneath. Glitching, dragged outro fades to silence., record scratching, very emotional, vocal-driven, eurodisco

bluefunk (Remix) v2c (Remix) 50 25 23Imagine a track where glitchy digital grit meets the sun-drenched soul of West Coast streets. Synths shimmer like heatwaves above pavement while deep basslines roll slow and steady like a cruising lowrider. But then the road shifts. A dusty Appalachian trail winds in. A banjo picks up pace like a train engine gaining speed while a fiddle wails and leaps in a fiery back-and-forth — their melodies chasing each other like wild stallions across open land. Underneath it all tribal kicks thud like heartbeats against packed earth. Double pulses echo like dancing boots on wooden porches. Foot stomps rise like thunder. Sharp finger snaps crack like branches in a storm. This is raw soul — not polished but alive. Born from a live acoustic session steeped in Southern Americana and soaked in the sweat of Gospel revival halls. The rhythm breathes with upright bass thumps and acoustic guitars that whisper then shout in layered textures. Brushes sweep across snares like wind through dry grass. Keys a

This Luv Izz 1216 (Remix)A Houston chopped and screwed hip hop track at 60 BPM with swung 4/4, where syncopated kicks and sub-bass drive offbeat grooves against a counter-melodic percussion line. Lush flanged pads and deeply reverbed drums sit under vinyl hiss. Every loop drops −1 dB and sweeps low-pass to 6 kHz, morphing further with tape-stop, reverse, scratch, and -2 st pitch FX. Loop-close sections are repeated with stepped-back effects, delivering hook tension and release, while a one-beat back-up sting creates scene change mid-song.

This Love Is Dangerous (Remix)this neo-West Coast G-Funk interpretation of Al Green-esque 1970's R&B/Trap-Soul mashup opens with vinyl-warped Rhodes, gliding 808 sub, wah-slide guitar, and gospel pads afloat in analog haze, Whisper-soft tenor vocals slip through intimate, crackling verses, while trap-soul drums lend lazy half-time sway, Choruses erupt with gospel choir, call-and-response, and lush whisper reverbs; wide, emotional leads soar over tape-echoed guitar and wet plate vocal layers, Dynamics ebb from hushed, breathy lows to explosive, choir-driven highs, closing on a gentle, analog-drenched fade

She's Cheatin' (On Us) (Remix)The song fuses a microtexas southern belle turned into a Southern Soul-infused groove—upright bass, brushed drums, rich acoustic guitars, and warm organ stabs—layered with zydeco accordion, banjo plucks, and hi-hat-heavy trap beats. A soulful low tenor lead trades verses with a commanding female vocal; both voices are doubled: the low tenor gets half-octave above and below harmonies set back for depth, while the female gets subtle harmonies mixed under. Accordion and guitar interplay fill breaks, crafting an intimate live vibe and dynamic pocket in a clean, wide, analog-warmed mix.

Country Booty Call (Council Rewrite – Elite Pen Game Edition)Genre: Fusion hip hop—Southern rap, outlaw country, modern bounce. Mood: Confident, playful, flirtatious, high-energy, bold. Voice: Female MC (agile, clever, rhythmic punch), Southern male MC or hype vocals, harmonized crowd chants, gang vocals. Tempo: 78–85 BPM, swung groove, dynamic sections (half-time bridge, bounce in verses).
